# Task 0963: to the start of P1

Close what stands between the built stages and P1 planning. The work runs in subagents; the orchestrator briefs, reviews, merges, gates and writes the handoff.

## Read on resume
Read only this file and `docs/HANDOFF.md`. Everything else is fetched when a step needs it, and by the subagent doing the step where possible:
- A decision by number (D1 to D87): `grep -n "^- D<n> " tasks/0338-s8-reach/PLAN.md`. Do not read that file whole; it is the record of the S8 task.
- A backlog line: `grep -n "^- \[.\] <id> " tasks/BACKLOG.md`.
- A spec section: `pnpm spec <topic>`. An RFC: the one file in `docs/rfc/`.
- A subagent's brief names the files it must read; the orchestrator does not read them first.

## Decisions
- D1 (2026-11-19): this task replaces 0338 as the active task so a resume reads a short plan. 0338's decisions stay in its plan and are cited by number.
- D2 (2026-11-19, operator): S6 is provisionally closed; the external a11y audit and pen test are P1 gates and S6 reopens if either fails.
- D3 (2026-11-19, operator): the return-link restriction (backlog 0956) is agreed.
- D4 (2026-11-19, operator): a session in a macOS or iOS shell is proven by automation first, since that repeats; a person at the machine is the fallback for a one-time check (backlog 0961).
- D5 (2026-11-19, operator): no `/clear` while a subagent runs; the handoff says when it is safe.
- D6 (2026-11-19): `tooling/smoke/shell-launch.mjs` gives a simulator 900 s to boot (was 300 s) and the `shell-ios` job 45 minutes (was 30). The hosted runner's first boot migrates data for about five minutes: three runs in a row failed E0014 at `Migration Elapsed:04:55`, and the one green run booted just under the bound.
- D7 (2026-11-19): in CI `@keel/cli` runs its test files on two vitest workers (`WORKERS` in `tooling/ci-shards.mjs`). `conformance/E1562-base-route-change.test.ts` spawns about twenty builds inside a 120 s test limit and took 117 s to 172 s for the file across four runs on the 4-core runner, failing T0001 in two; the limit is in a conformance file, so the load is lowered instead. If the file still nears its limit with two workers, the limit needs an `rfc:` change.

- D8 (2026-11-19): RFC 0147 and ADR-0169 (backlog 0956) are accepted as written by the design lane. An opening is one external-browser open by a mount request; it lasts 600000 ms, is kept in `ctx.storage` `keel.return` so a relaunch during sign-in keeps it, admits one link, and a new one replaces it. No binding value: the library's `state` and PKCE bind the flow. Two consequences for the operator to confirm: a link to a page needs an opening too (the agreed wording covers every return link, so the scheme is no general deep link into the app), and a flow started outside the app (an emailed link) cannot return to it. Residual risk in the threat model: another app can send its link first while an opening is pending.

- D9 (2026-11-19): the first wave of `tasks/p1/PLAN.md` starts before the freeze review closes, on the operator's word to run more lanes at once: weakness triage under the O1 definition, release hygiene and the evidence gates. None touches a kernel section.

- D10 (2026-11-19): the red-team pass on RFC 0147 reproduced two holes, so the branch is not merged as built. One opening admitted any number of links, because an admitted link's own GET recorded a new opening (a 404, an outside redirect); and a redirect to the shell's own scheme restarted the redirect count, so a loop never ended. Decided: only a request the page starts, and its redirects, record an opening; a chain started by a return link records none; an own-scheme redirect continues the row of five. The spec also states the far-future and malformed stored value and the failed write. Order: spec text, conformance, build, then a second red-team pass.

- D11 (2026-11-19): the amended return-link text does not fit `k.return`'s size limit, so the chain and stored-value rules are a section of their own, `k.opening`, as RFC 0130 and 0138 did for full sections; eleven map-only titles are shortened to pay for its row.

- D12 (2026-11-19): RFC 0147 merges with backlog 0968 open. The branch closes the hole the operator agreed to close and both holes of the first red-team pass; 0968 needs app code that navigates to the mount with no person acting, and gets an RFC of its own. Spec text for an RFC stays on its lane branch until its conformance exists: the RFC 0147 text on the main line lowered three ledger items and failed CI's `check`.

- D13 (2026-11-19, operator): the golden-3 agent run waits until the agent docs and skills of the P1 plan (workstream W5) exist, so the run tests what a public user's agent will read. W5 moves ahead of golden-3; the S6 exit proof and the audit target wait with it.
- D14 (2026-11-19, operator): the return-link restriction as built (RFC 0147, and RFC 0150 on `lane-w03`) is too strict; the operator reviews alternatives before more is built. The RFC 0150 build is stopped with uncommitted work in `.worktrees/w03`. RFC 0147 stays on the main line, unpushed, until the design is settled. This reverses D3 and D8.
- D15 (2026-11-19, operator): no licence until the public release: `LICENSE` states all rights reserved and the root package is `UNLICENSED` (backlog 0941 closed; the choice of licence is made at launch).
- D16 (2026-11-19, operator): the first public release is a 0.x preview. The kernel freeze is tried out during it, with the right to reopen before 1.0 and the intent not to need it. `tasks/p1/PLAN.md` is updated to this.
- D17 (2026-11-19): lane 02 (server scrub) merges after its red-team pass (verdict merge): backlog 0671 and 0683 fixed, 0722, 0737 and 0441 proven or stated as limits. Open from it: 0442 and 0756 (the server is 5 bytes under its size budget; 0756 also contradicts a conformance test), 0729 (the 1.2 s cost did not reproduce: about 155 ms), and new lines 0970 to 0974.

- D18 (2026-11-19, operator): the return-link design, replacing D3, D8 and the tap rule of RFC 0150. (1) A link to a page always opens it: the app's scheme is a general deep link, with the exposure of a web link. (2) A link to a path under the identity mount is accepted while an opening is pending (OAuth returns), or at any time when the app has declared that path as safe to receive from outside (a magic-link or verify-email callback); every other mount path is refused. (3) A sign-in that an incoming link set off, until a person next acts, records no opening, so its return is refused; a sign-in auto-started at an ordinary launch or by a tap works. A cold start whose launch is itself a link counts as link-triggered.

- D19 (2026-11-19): the std-sessions suite removes the notes fixture's `.keel` when it ends (`test/removeFixtureArtifacts.ts`, a vitest global setup). A group user writes compiler artifacts beside the fixture's Vite config; left on disk they read as a project of the workspace, so the committed ledger held 80 `a11y:notes.*` items a clean checkout lacks, and CI's `check` failed S0011 on a1df93cd (its `test` job failed only because the shards were skipped). A stale `packages/std-sessions/.stryker-tmp` sandbox doubled them and was deleted. The ledger command is left as the spec states it (every `routes.json` is a project); a ledger written on a machine with a mutation sandbox or a fixture's artifacts on disk is wrong, which is why the Method writes it from a clean checkout.

- D20 (2026-11-19): weakness lane 01 is merged (RFC 0148 and 0149, ADR-0170 and 0171). The `@keel/std-offline` size budget moves from 3.5 kB to 3.7 kB (3,759 B measured): the per-ctx outbox state and the same-seed and drain-end fixes of its red-team pass. Backlog 0800 stays open and needs an RFC.

- D21 (2026-11-19): two questions the RFC 0150 spec lane left. No development warning when a link-started chain opens the browser: `@keel/testing` is already over its size budget on that lane and the refusal is observable in a test; the idea goes to `tasks/FUTURE.md` if a user trips on it. No std auth adapter supplies its library's `links`: the app declares each mount path a link may open, so what is reachable from outside is written in the app and checked by E1584.

- D22 (2026-11-19): k.opening's bullet "a second at 300000 ms with a `ctx.storage` whose writes now fail" contradicts k.links' "takes the pending opening, in one `commit`" when every write fails (judged from the spec by the conformance-writer). Decided, fail closed: the bullet's failing write is the one that records the second opening, and the take's `commit` still works, so the link at 599999 ms takes the older opening; a link whose take cannot commit sends nothing. The spec names the write that records an opening, the bullet is reworded under rfc:0150, and the test fails only that write. Applied on `lane-w03` with the red-team fixes.

- D23 (2026-11-19): the red-team pass on the RFC 0150 build (verdict: merge after fixes). F1, high, reproduced: a launch report with a link that is not taken, or a failed read, left the app able to record an opening with no act, which a second link takes. Decided: any launch report that is not "no links" makes the app link-started until a person acts. F2, medium, reproduced and older than this build: a mount answer `Location: /.//host/x` opened another origin in the external browser; a path beginning `//` or `/\` after normalisation is ignored. F3 is backlog 0976. F4 and the declared path's redirects are stated as limits. Order on `lane-w03`: spec text, conformance, build, a second red-team pass on F1 and F2.

- D24 (2026-11-19): weakness lane 07 wrote three kernel RFCs on `lane-w07`, each a k.schema change, taken under RFC defaults 4 and 6 for the operator to confirm or reverse: RFC 0152 / ADR-0174 (the blank set of `required` is `White_Space`, `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, `Cc`, `Cf`, `Cs`, `M` and U+2800, with a JSON Schema `pattern` of `\u` ranges only), RFC 0153 / ADR-0175 (a `Secret` under an undeclared key of an open object fails `type`; chosen over stripping undeclared keys at every commit), RFC 0154 / ADR-0176 (`piiPaths` lists `<object>.*` for a marked open object; std-sessions `redact` then needs a follow-up). k.schema went over its 1,500-token limit with their text, so Format types move to a section of their own, as k.opening did; the section budget is not raised. `@keel/schema` is 3 bytes under its size budget and the 0152 and 0154 builds are estimated to pass it by about 130 bytes: decided at the build with measured numbers.

- D25 (2026-11-19): the operator said to keep working with the open questions unanswered, so each goes ahead on the orchestrator's recommendation, on lane branches that are not pushed, for the operator to confirm or reverse: ADR-0173 as written (`keel docs` replaces `keel spec`; seven published skills), RFC 0151's design choices as the lane made them, RFC 0152 to 0154, the 0690 and 0867 RFCs as lane 09 recommends (not started), E1107 kept narrower than the shared reach and stated (0880, not started), the blank-text rule left in three places until after the freeze (0804), the `bearer` guidance in the skill, and RFC 0142 with ADR-0164 marked accepted (not done yet).
- D26 (2026-11-19): the spec map (`spec/00-map.md`) is at exactly its 1,200-token budget on `lane-w07` after the `k.formats` row, and `lane-w03` (`k.links`) and `lane-w5l1` (`k.docs`) each add a row with their own title shortenings. They will conflict; the map is merged by hand at each lane's merge, and if no more titles can shrink the map budget is re-based with the row count as the reason.

- D27 (2026-11-19): the second red-team pass on RFC 0150 confirms F1 and F2 closed (verdict: merge after fixes, nothing high or medium). Fixed on the lane: a malformed link threw and stopped delivery of later launch links; the parser's stand-in origin `keel.invalid` was reachable through `//`; the spec said any other URL opens in the external browser where only `http:` and `https:` do; a reload starts a ctx that is not link-started (stated as a limit); `personActs.mark` leaves the router's internal entry. No third pass: each fix refuses more, and the lane's conformance and router suite gate the merge.
- D28 (2026-11-19): RFC 0154's lookup rule, as amended: a path is followed through declared fields and takes its nearest mark's class, so a declared non-leaf (`address.geo`) has a class; `<object>.*` covers only undeclared keys, at any depth. Chosen over "no class" for a declared non-leaf because that would leave a control bound to it unmasked.

- D29 (2026-11-19): RFC 0150 is merged as three commits (RFC and spec, conformance, build) by a squash of `lane-w03`, since the lane held `wip:` commits and conflicted with the day's merges. k.mount, k.return, k.opening and k.links are `conformance-covered`. `@keel/testing`'s size budget moves from 13.5 kB to 13.6 kB (13,855 B measured: the `launch` handle and the `fire` wrapper the spec states; two restructurings came out larger). The longer status word put the map and k.mount over their limits, paid for with three map-only titles and one k.mount sentence. The published keel-server and keel-testing skills do not yet name `identityMount`'s `links` or the `launch` handle (a line for the next skills pass).
- D30 (2026-11-19): the seven published skills and the docs checks are on the main line before `keel docs` exists there, because the skills lane and the docs checks were ready and RFC 0151's build is still running. Until RFC 0151 merges, an agent working in this repo that follows a skill's `keel docs <id>` uses `pnpm spec <id>`.
- D31 (2026-11-19): pnpm 11.17.0 denies the build scripts of `@biomejs/biome` and `esbuild` (`allowBuilds: false` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`); both run from their platform packages, checked by running each binary after a clean install.

- D32 (2026-11-19): the review of the RFC 0152 to 0154 build (verdict: merge after one fix). `piiClassAt` took only the first union variant that declares a segment, so a field marked in a later variant lost its class and its mask: being fixed on the lane with a property test that the lookup never states less than `piiPaths`. Kept after probing: `hasSecret` reading the node's JSON text (no false negative or positive found; it only decides sealing). Decided: an undeclared path under a closed object takes the class in force, as the code does, reversing that clause of D28: it over-masks and forms refuses such a path first (E1902); the RFC and spec are amended to agree. Backlog 0980 is the `Map`, `Set` and symbol-key gap of RFC 0153.

- D33 (2026-11-19): the push of b2c08fd5 broke 15 cli tests. Lane 05's rule that refused every type reference but `node` (0884) contradicted `cli/conformance/boundary-env.test.ts` and `build-production-mode.test.ts`, whose project code types `import.meta.env` through `vite/client`; the lane and its review ran the check suite only, and the pre-push gate did not run the cli suite. The rule is removed and 0884 reopened for an RFC. The Method changes: before a push the full `pnpm test` runs in the clean checkout, and a lane that changes `@keel/check`, `@keel/compiler` or `@keel/schema` runs the cli suite, since cli conformance exercises them end to end.
- D34 (2026-11-19): with the seven skills listed, `keel docs` prints about 1,400 tokens against the 1,200 of `docs.map`. Not raised: a skill entry in the listing prints its description up to the last `;` (the codes after it are for a host matching the skill); the skill itself prints verbatim. Stated in k.docs and RFC 0151 on the lane.

- D35 (2026-11-19): RFC 0152 to 0154 are merged as three commits by a squash of `lane-w07` (0469, 0261, 0595, 0801, 0802, 0287 closed). Two budgets moved, each after being squeezed more than once today: the spec map from 1,200 to 1,250 tokens (four sections joined it: k.opening, k.links, k.formats, and k.docs to come; its titles were shortened four times), and `@keel/router` from 11.25 kB to 11.3 kB (11,527 B measured: the link intake of RFC 0150 and the blank pattern in the routes artifact). `docs.map`, the budget of what `keel docs` prints, stays at 1,200 (D34). The full `pnpm test` in the clean checkout found a second fault of the lane 05 merge: a helper named `listed` in `check/src/internal/importedFunction.ts` tripped `server/test/inspectSingleDefinition.test.ts`, which reads every package for a second definition of the inspect origin matcher; renamed.
- For the next skills pass (the published skills are at their size targets, so each needs a trade): keel-compliance should say an open object fails `parse` at a `Secret` under an undeclared key; keel-client that `decodeStrings` drops a blank value of an optional `min: 1` string; keel-server and keel-testing `identityMount`'s `links` and the `launch` handle (D29).

- D36 (2026-11-19): weakness lane 04 (server edges) closes six lines with no kernel change: 0364, 0350, 0274 and 0865 were already built or covered (each now pinned by a test or a recorded smoke run), 0503 and 0415 are stated as limits in the threat model (no per-caller share of event streams, since a caller cookie is free to mint, so the remedy is rate limiting at the proxy; an API route's 405 answers before its guards and so tells any client the path's write methods). 0569 gets RFC 0155 / ADR-0177: the server never serves a `*.map` under `/assets/`, whatever built the folder. k.deploy has three characters of room, so the conformance case is stated in the RFC and ADR.

- D37 (2026-11-19): `docs.map` moves from 1,200 to 1,300 tokens at the RFC 0151 merge, reversing the "not raised" of D34. With the description cut at its last `;` the listing measures 1,254: about 850 for the 87 section lines and about 400 for the seven skills. It lists what the spec map lists (1,250 since D35) plus the skills, so it cannot be smaller than that map without dropping the package column an agent needs to know what to install. A fix lane measured a load average near 50 with four test lanes and a gate running: at most two test-running lanes beside a gate from here on.

- D38 (2026-11-19): the review of the RFC 0151 build (verdict: merge after fixes). An id cannot steer `keel docs` to an arbitrary file, but both sides followed symlinks: a `docs/` folder linked out of an installed package was read, and with `.claude` linked out of the project `keel docs --sync` wrote skills there. Decided: a docs or skill file whose real path is outside its package's real folder is absent; a write whose nearest existing ancestor resolves outside the project, or whose target is a symlink, is refused with a diagnostic. Also: a package whose `name` is not `@keel/<folder>` is not installed, a file over 1,000,000 bytes is absent, the block keeps the file's line ending, and the scaffold tests stop packing `packages/cli/docs` in place (an interrupted or parallel run left or deleted it). The reviewer did not check E1065, the `see:` lines, the `;` cut or the MCP budget; conformance covers those.

- D39 (2026-11-20): the review of weakness lane 03 (`lane-w03b`). Not merged as built. Keep: `identityMount` throwing E1580 for a malformed `path` (0894) and `splitUrl` as one regex (identical on 25,000 generated strings, no backtracking). Change before a merge: the set of mount paths is module-level state that only grows, so one app's or test's mount changes another's routing in the same process (principle 2): scope it to the router or the app. Drop the E1579 refusal of param routes: it refuses a legitimate `/:locale/:slug` beside a mount at `/api/auth`, and the router refusing to match under the mount already closes the hole. `settle`'s 2,000-turn bound reads any pending deadline, not one the page waits on, so a debounce beside a slow import could trip it: tie it to what the settle waits for or restore the old bound, and name the unit in the message. Three behaviours have no spec sentence and go in one RFC (0156): the run-time throw of E1580, no page matched at or under a mount path, a path holding a backslash matching no page.
- D40 (2026-11-20): on the RFC 0151 lane, a refused write refuses the whole set, keeping k.scaffold's all-or-none promise (the builder's D9), and takes the code E1357. The scaffold conformance spawns the real cli, which reads `packages/cli/docs/map.json` beside itself, so those tests need the cli's docs packed in place: the test helper packs them when absent and never removes them (the folder is ignored by git and read by no gate), which ends the race of one run deleting what another needs. A refused write through the MCP path must answer E1357 as the binary does.
- D41 (2026-11-21): the operator's review memo of 2026-11-20 was checked against the tree and discussed. The freeze flip (W1 of `tasks/p1/PLAN.md`) waits for four RFCs that change app-facing shape: the module contract (D42), the component signature `(props, ctx)` with `ctx` required, declared third-party origins (D46, if the operator accepts it) and the k.shell split (D47, if accepted). Additive work does not gate the flip: `pick`, `omit`, `extend`, `partial`, a cross-field check with a field path, param names inferred from the path, a schema family in one file. The memo's stale figures: 475 of 616 kernel clauses are at the example rung; 5 of 8 model eval runs converged.
- D42 (2026-11-21, operator): one contract per module, required before the work ends and before the flip. A client-safe file owned by the serving module declares each endpoint once; the server binds handlers to it and the client service, links and the public surface derive from it. It makes E1561 unrepresentable in one build (a missing or mistyped handler is a type error); E1562 stays, since it compares a build with the deployed base, and reads one object. It reopens RFC 0086 D2. E1504 forbids a required query key on an API route today; the RFC settles that. ADR-0052 gives a TypeScript circular initializer as the reason for `routes.actions()`; the RFC states whether the contract removes it. Workstream W13 of `tasks/p1/PLAN.md`.
- D43 (2026-11-21, operator): app data is not the framework's. The kernel validates what crosses a boundary and does not store or fetch app data, as it does not sign people in: the app plugs in any library or store (a Postgres client, Mongo, an in-house library) and Keel checks only that the types line up. `read` and `commit` (k.persist) stay as the storage of Keel's own state. A Postgres stdlib module is the included battery and the worked example, and the one exception to "no new stdlib packages". Its first scope (the orchestrator's): a capability on `ctx` with a real and a simulated side, a tagged `sql` template, a result schema on every query, PGlite in dev, tests and simulation, forward-only SQL migrations. Waiting until a second store asks for them: a store-neutral core, a seed engine, the build-time SQL check, row ownership. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the golden apps follow the RFC. Required before the first real apps. Workstream W14.
- D44 (2026-11-21, operator): no charts in the framework, and no component that makes a library choice for every app. The framework gives building blocks and a blessed way to write a custom component: from the base elements, from the primitives stdlib widgets build on (`@keel/ui/aria` `Part`, which E1822 keeps from app code today), and around an outside library or a standard custom element, bound to data efficiently and correctly (resume, teardown, a text alternative, the harness). Charts are then a component library or an app's own wrapper over a chart library or d3. Imperative for real apps. A design RFC first; workstream W15. The memo's rich text, canvas element and upload go to `tasks/FUTURE.md` unless W15 or a real app needs them.
- D45 (2026-11-21, operator): the licence stays as O4 decides; the operator grants permission to an outside party where needed. The head-to-head eval starts small: one pair of runs, then two more, then a decision on the larger set. Codex (local CLI, subscription) and Claude Code are both available, so the second vendor's model costs no new account. The orchestrator's choices, to confirm or reverse: the incumbent arm is Next.js with its usual libraries; the first spec is the golden-2 catalog task with a hidden acceptance suite; the second and third runs are the same pair under Codex and one maintenance task (add a field to an existing app). Workstream W16.
- D46 (2026-11-21, accepted by the operator: safe by default, opened by config): third-party origins declared per deployable in `topology.ts`, one directive at a time, each an exact origin with a reason, listed in the evidence bundle; the default stays the fixed policy. First directives: `img`, `media`, `font`, `connect`, `frame`. A third-party script is its own, later decision, since `'strict-dynamic'` ignores host lists and Trusted Types refuses a script that writes HTML strings. `style-src` and Trusted Types stay absolute. It reverses the "no option" sentence of k.security and ADR-0094, so the RFC lands before the flip. A public API needs none of this: the policy governs what a Keel document loads, and callers from other origins are `KEEL_ALLOW_ORIGINS` and `bearer`.
- D47 (2026-11-21, accepted by the operator): split k.shell. The kernel keeps the mechanism (a `client` deployable declares packaging targets a package provides; `mode: 'shell'` and the driver contract; E1573, E1574); the Capacitor and Tauri files, toolchain versions, build steps and CI jobs move to std.shells, where they follow those vendors' releases. No feature leaves 1.0 and both shells stay exercised. It removes the k.shell freeze blocker.
- D48 (2026-11-21, operator): the shape of W15. `Part` opens to app code (E1822 goes). No drawing element and no managed set of SVG shapes: a static SVG is an `Image`; a live drawing (d3, a canvas chart library, a standard custom element) lives in one host element, whose node the outside code owns. The host's contract (the orchestrator's proposal, for the RFC): a `Props` schema, a required text alternative and role, client-only, `mount`, `update` and `dispose` driven by signals, opaque to tests and replay, counted in the ledger. A custom element is the host's thin case, not a second mechanism. The fixed policy stays: a library that injects `<style>` or writes HTML strings does not run, which the RFC states after trying real libraries (d3, Chart.js, uPlot, Observable Plot, ECharts) and recording which parts of each work. Behaviour primitives beside `Part` are only those the stdlib widgets already hold privately.
- D49 (2026-11-21, proposed to the operator, who asked for a blessed way to publish Keel components with the guarantees built in): a Keel library is a package kind. Proposal for the W15 RFC: it ships source and a manifest (its surface, token names it needs, ledger rungs, `unsafe` and host-element counts, packed docs for `keel docs`); the app's `keel check` and compiler process it as app code, so the guarantees are checked again and not taken on trust; `keel scaffold library` and a publish check that refuses a library failing its own gate. The stdlib uses the same format and holds no power a library lacks. To verify first: how the compiler and check treat `node_modules` today (E1822 exempts it, so an outside package can import `Part` unchecked).
- D50 (2026-11-21, operator): `Part` gains the roles an app needs to build the widget patterns the stdlib does not ship: `grid` and `gridcell` at least, and `slider`, `tree` with `treeitem`, and `radiogroup` with `radio` if the RFC finds each a primitive and not a widget. The framework ships no date picker. W15 has two worked examples, each built as an app component in a golden app: a date picker (a `Dialog` with a `grid`, arrow, Home, End and Page keys, one tab stop) for the open `Part`, and a d3 chart for the host element. An app component is already the unit of reuse (a `Props` contract, function props for content, `<id>.<part>` ids, `styles`); a library (D49) only packages it. D49 goes ahead on the orchestrator's recommendation, the operator not having answered: designed in the W15 RFC before the flip, built after the first real apps.

- D51 (2026-11-21): the push of the ledger commit and the plan commits after 2f7f64e7 is gated in `.worktrees/verify` by `pnpm check`, `pnpm ledger --diff` (0 lowered, 0 uncovered) and the evidence check on the head; the full `pnpm test` and build are not repeated, since nothing but `tasks/`, `docs/` and `tooling/ledger/` changed since the commit they passed on. A full `pnpm test` in the main tree failed one task for a local reason: `apps/golden-2/node_modules/.bin` held stale `vitest`, `tsx` and `esbuild` shims from an older install, which a frozen install does not remove; deleted by hand, golden-2's 134 tests pass. A test run piped through `tail` hides its exit status: read the last `ok` or `fail` line.
- D52 (2026-11-21): RFC 0151's conformance found the `CLAUDE.md` bullet at odds with "keeps a `CLAUDE.md`". Decided: an existing `CLAUDE.md` is kept and never written, linked or not, so nothing is refused and the linked file is byte for byte unchanged; a `CLAUDE.md` that is a dangling symlink would be created through the link, so it is refused with E1357 like any linked target. The MCP `docs` tool's `sync` argument is stated in k.docs as the build has it, and its refusal carries E1357.

- D53 (2026-11-21): RFC 0160 / ADR-0182 (declared third-party origins) is written on `lane-r160` (6f8b565d, e2457f1c): a new section `k.origins`, codes E1590 and E1591, `origins` on any deployable, and a `Frame` base element in k.ui (required `title`, fixed `sandbox`), since E1805 leaves `frame` unusable without one. Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator to confirm or reverse: `Frame` stays in this RFC; same-origin media (the policy has no `media-src`) and a `Video` or `Audio` element wait for a k.ui RFC after W15; third-party scripts wait for a real need. k.ui is at exactly 6,000 characters there, so RFC 0159's k.ui text conflicts at the merge. Next: conformance from k.origins' eleven bullets, then the build (`@keel/server` is 5 bytes under its budget).

- D54 (2026-11-21): RFC 0161 / ADR-0183 (the k.shell split) is written on `lane-r161` (43d66b6e): the kernel keeps `shells` as the closed set `capacitor | tauri`, `mode: 'shell'`, the driver contract and the build steps; a package provides a target through `shellTarget(shell, target)` in its `./tools` entry; new sections std.capacitor and std.tauri hold the vendor files, versions and runs; new code E1585; glossary term "shell target". Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator: the shell set stays closed for 1.0 (a third-party shell package would need `@keel/router/internal`'s `mountRequests`, which is not public; opening the set later is additive, though it sits beside D49's "the stdlib holds no power a library lacks" as a stated exception), and the generated iOS job stays `manual`. Next: conformance (files move word for word to std-tauri and std-capacitor, three change, kernel bullets run over a stub target), then the build.

- D55 (2026-11-21): RFC 0158 / ADR-0180 (app data outside the kernel) is written on `lane-r158` (9849a60e, 5a30f015, 59eaac65): a new kernel section `k.data` and a std section `std.postgres` (full at 1,496 tokens), package `@keel/std-postgres`. The seam is the existing `register<T>`: an app capability lives in one `*.server.ts` file under `<moduleId>.<name>` (new rule E1607), with a sim side; `ctx.storage` keeps Keel's own state only; an action writes to stores or to app data, never both; `database(name, { migrations })`, a `sql(schema)` tag, forward-only migrations per module, `KEEL_POSTGRES_URL` (`postgres:` or `pglite:`), a PGlite database per sim ctx; codes E2451 to E2458. It assumes of RFC 0157 only that a handler receives `ctx` and returns a `Result` its `output` schema encodes. Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator: no rule against records kept in a server store yet; better-auth's own database stays outside simulation; a shared fake-against-real scenario test goes to `tasks/FUTURE.md`; std.tokens gets an additive `idOf(token)` RFC (backlog 0984). Conformance (k.data 6 clauses, std.postgres 8) waits for W13 step 2. Backlog 0981 to 0985 hold what the RFC lanes found in the tree.

- D56 (2026-11-21): the four small RFCs are written on `lane-r164` (495e62b2, cde13e6a, fcaee5b7, 7f06706a; 4d3026b2 marks RFC 0142 and ADR-0164 accepted). RFC 0164 (0690): k.privacy Sinks lists the scrubbed forms; the `req` path rule moves word for word to a k.serverlog Path paragraph. RFC 0165 (0867): a recording holds no transaction `meta` at all, so no `meta.caller` under any lifetime; this reverses RFC 0139's "without the option, the hash". RFC 0166 (0880): k.leave states E1107's own reach (component files and their relative imports, never through a `*.server.*` file). RFC 0167 (0884): a Keel-owned types-only entry `@keel/compiler/client` declares the five `import.meta.env` keys and the `?worker`, `?sharedworker` and `?url` imports with `strictImportMetaEnv`; `vite/client` stays allowed. Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator: the forms list is a minimum the scrub may exceed; a store an island finds only by `stores()` id is a stated limit of E1107; `?raw` and `?inline` stay undeclared. Next: conformance (new tests in std-sessions, server and check; four header retags; `boundary-env.test.ts` reads its probe keys through `Reflect.get`), then a build lane that runs the cli suite (D33).

- D57 (2026-11-21): RFC 0159 / ADR-0181 (custom components) and RFC 0163 / ADR-0185 (the library format) are written on `lane-r159` (df096bf5, 3e244074; the library trial's scripts and results are in `tasks/p1/w15-csp-trial/` there). `Part` opens and E1822 is retired; new section k.roles (`grid` with `gridcell`, `tree` with `treeitem`; `slider` and `radio` only as the native inputs, since a `div` in those roles rebuilds a native control); std.lists opens `listChart` and `stableRows`; D48's host element is named `Foreign` (glossary: foreign element; section k.foreign), because `host` already means a serving deployable; its code loads only by a literal dynamic import (E1824), reports back through a schema-typed `emit`, and is never run by the harness or replay; a stylesheet import is allowed only in a foreign code file (E1825). Under the production policy in Chromium and WebKit: d3 runs but for `.html()` and the style attribute, Chart.js and uPlot run fully, Observable Plot draws without its injected style and ramp legends, ECharts runs only its SVG renderer. A library (k.library, E1161 to E1164) is a package marked `"keel": "library"` shipping source and a `keel-library.json` written by `keel pack`; the app compiles and checks it as app code. Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator: the name `Foreign`; no Trusted Types policy for libraries (ECharts canvas stays out); `Dialog` gets no initial-focus prop; a library may have `./tools`. One recommendation changes kernel surface and is the operator's to confirm before it is built: the eight kernel `internal` imports of the stdlib become public seams, so that the stdlib holds no power a library lacks (it meets D54's closed shell set, which rests on one of those imports). Found in the tree: the compiler skips `node_modules` and `keel check` never reads it, and no rule stops a `.css` import from app code today. Conformance for k.roles, k.foreign and std.lists starts when a test lane frees; k.library is built after the first real apps (D49).
- D58 (2026-11-21): a conformance-writer may learn a public API's types from a package's `src/index.ts`, its built `.d.ts`, its README and neighbouring conformance files; one stopped on RFC 0156 after reading type files under `src/types/`, and was re-run fresh.

- D59 (2026-11-21): RFC 0157 / ADR-0179 (the module contract) and RFC 0162 / ADR-0184 (the component signature) are written on `lane-r157` (1fe80fd6, 12f3c284). Each module has one client-safe `modules/<id>/<id>.contract.ts` exporting `contract('<id>', {...})` with page, layout and API entries keyed by full route id, one id per method; `routes.ts` becomes `manifest(contract, binding)` with every entry bound; handlers name their entry and take their types from it (E1507, E1512 retired); links are `<id>.href`, clients `service({ contract })`; `routes.json` and `surface.json` (v2) are written from the contract; `routes.actions`, `service({ module })` and `public.routes` go. E1561 is removed; E1562 compares contract entries; E1504 applies to a page's `search` only and an API `query` may require keys (422 after the guard). The contract removes ADR-0052's TS7022 cycle (checked with tsc 5.9.3), and a typed prototype over golden-2's kitchen and recipes schemas compiles while eight wrong bindings fail. Components are `(props, ctx)` with `ctx` required; the `slots` argument goes. New codes E1513 to E1515 and E1407; `keel codemod contract <module>` migrates. Going ahead on the lane's recommendations, for the operator to read before the build starts (W13 says so): pages are in the contract; full route ids as keys; one id per method (`kitchen.recipes` becomes `kitchen.box` and `kitchen.add`); `public.routes` removed; E1562 covers API entries only; runtime ops take no ctx; a component that reaches no effect may leave `ctx` out. At the merge: ADR-0052 marked superseded, RFC 0086 D2 reopened, `tasks/FUTURE.md` line 7 removed, and the codes of the five RFC lanes checked against each other. Conformance from k.contract starts when a test lane frees; the build waits for the operator's read.
- D60 (2026-11-21): the second review of `lane-w03b` (verdict: merge after fixes). Decided: (1) the mount test is textual and case-sensitive on the undecoded path, on client and server alike, and k.mount says so with the limit that a proxy which decodes or lowercases paths must not sit in front (`/%67ate`, `/GATE` render a page and the server's handle does not take them either); (2) RFC 0156's "raw or after its other checks" becomes the raw backslash only, as built; (3) a hand-wired `createApp` whose `handle.path` differs from the root `identity.ts` is a stated limit, tied by `keel check` (E1580); (4) no rule k.mount stated before the lane may be lost to make room: a trimmed sentence that carried a rule (cookies, root-relative, `/magic-link/verify`) is restored, and the new text moves to a section with room if need be; (5) the shell's `mountRequests` still reading the process-wide set is the same fault at lower reach (shells only) and is fixed with RFC 0161's build, which reshapes `shellMount` and can change the k.return conformance under its own `rfc:`; backlog 0988. A fix lane applies 1 to 4 after the conformance-writer, then the merge.

- D61 (2026-11-21): RFC 0151 is on the main line as three commits (80d90ce1 RFC and spec, fa55387f conformance, 9ae9c047 build); `keel docs` replaces `keel spec`, k.docs is a section and W0013 is gone. The `@keel/cli` size budget moves from 25.75 kB to 26 kB (26,545 B measured): the docs command, the host docs reader and the MCP `docs` tool with its refused-write answer; the lane was already over before its last fixes. The map is at 1,216 of 1,250 tokens. `E1562-base-route-change` failed once in the merge worktree's cli run under a load near 60 with a socket error and passes alone.

- D62 (2026-11-21): the review of the RFC 0161 build (verdict: merge after fixes; generated files verified byte-identical for golden-1, no cli in a browser bundle, no cycle). Decided: (1) `keel check` and `keel build` import a shell package's `./tools` only for a shell the topology declares, never because a path or text matches a shell's name, and the threat model's stdlib-loading paragraph states this import; (2) a target's `files()` output is untrusted: a path whose target or nearest existing ancestor resolves outside the deployable's shell folder, or whose target is a symlink, is E1585, the rule of D38; (3) the registered target is held per project root, not per process, so an LSP or MCP serving two projects reads each one's own, and a second registration reports E1053, not E1159; (4) `shellPolicy` staying in `@keel/cli` is named in the RFC as kept, reached through `ShellApp.policy`; (5) `identityMounts` stays process-wide for `renderRoute` alone, as a k.return clause states it, and 0988 is closed with that noted. E1052 exits 2 as k.cli's usage codes say; the two tests that expected 1 are corrected. The `@keel/cli` budget is settled on the merged tree.

- D63 (2026-11-21): pushed e967c745 after two red gates and a green one. The reds: std-report expected the old `see:` text (rfc:0151); two `// Spec:` quotes drifted when build commits reworded their sentences, and one held backslash-escaped quotes that never matched (backlog 0992); a gate agent ran the apps' `vitest run` where `keel test` writes the a11y evidence. The gate's order is: build, both apps' `keel test`, `pnpm ledger`, `pnpm evidence:report`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm ledger --diff`, the evidence check, then the full `pnpm test`; the ledger files are committed and `pnpm check` runs once more on that commit. W5 L7 (the eval's public mode and the packed kit) is merged; the golden-3 agent run is the operator's to start, in a container or with a throwaway home, since the runner starts the agent with permission prompts off and the audit reads the transcript afterwards.

- D64 (2026-11-21): the author of RFC 0157 settled the ten points its first conformance pass could not test (2382b82c, 601e7512 on `lane-r157`; new sections k.routes and std.contracts; the map at 4,935 of 5,000 characters). `routeTable(...tables)` from `@keel/router` joins modules' manifests and throws E1502 and E1509 across them; a binding names its parent with `layout?` and `manifest` throws E1506; k.routes holds one table of where each code fires; k.compat states `surface.json` v2; `contract` lives in a new entry `@keel/router/contract` that imports `@keel/schema` only (budget 2 kB), so a service client never pulls the router in; the server render's ctx is the `createApp({ ctx })` object itself; `channelEntries` goes in the contract and `channelRoute` in the binding, since a contract cannot import a `*.server.ts` file. Added to the operator's list of D59, each recommended: the import path `@keel/router/contract`; a failing channel `params` answers 422, not 404; the codemod names an API route of several methods `<m>.<method><Name>`, GET included. A second conformance pass finishes the rewrite (about 130 files) and the new clauses.

- D65 (2026-11-21, operator): (1) no migration before P1: nothing is released and the golden apps are the only apps, so an RFC carries no codemod, no old-version compatibility case and no migration notes; the lane rewrites the golden apps, fixtures and skills directly. RFC 0157 loses `keel codemod contract`, the `surface.json` version 1 base case (W1568) and its Migration section; RFC 0161, 0162 and 0167 lose theirs. (2) Sizes: a lane does not hunt bytes; an overrun is re-based to the measured number and recorded, and one review before P1 trims and sets real budgets (backlog 0993). Spec section limits and the map budget stay as they are (the orchestrator's reading). (3) The host element of D48 is `Host`, glossary term "host element"; `Foreign` is dropped for its connotation. (4) Replies are concise, and correct first.

- D66 (2026-11-21): the red-team pass on the RFC 0160 merge (`merge-0160`; verdict: merge after fixes; the origin validator held against every injection tried, and `connect` widens `connect-src` only). H1, high, reproduced: a spread inside a deployable (`...{ origins }`) or a second `origins` key is skipped by the static read and honoured at run time, so the served policy holds an origin that `security.json`, `csp.txt` and the check rules never saw. Decided, fail closed on both sides: a spread, a computed key or a repeated key inside a deployable's declaration is a diagnostic from the static read, and the server emits only origins the static read produced, so the two cannot differ. M1: a `frame` origin equal to the app's own origin defeats the sandbox (`allow-scripts allow-same-origin`): refused where the origin is known (a peer or host origin; `localhost` at the dev port), `Frame` renders `about:blank` for a `src` of the document's own origin, and the threat model says a frame is sandboxed only against another origin. Low, fixed in the same lane: a reason needs a visible character by RFC 0152's blank set and holds no control or format character; an IPv6 literal is E1590 (CSP has no host-source form for it); one directive written twice is E1591; E1570 reads `/\host` and `\\host` as the absolute URLs browsers make of them; E1805 on a raw `<iframe>` suggests `Frame`. Backlog 0994: `declaredFor` follows relative imports only. Budgets on that branch are re-based, not squeezed (D65).

- D67 (2026-11-21, operator): (1) the topology key `host` becomes `servedBy`, so "host" in the API means only the host element; RFC 0168 / ADR-0190 on `lane-r168`. (2) Pages stay in the contract; with it the D59 and D64 list goes ahead and the W13 build may start. (3) The stdlib's kernel `internal` imports: remove the need where the stdlib can be reworked onto public API, and give a public form where a library needs the power (`secretPathOf`, `findRouter`, `report`); `mountRequests` stays internal while the shell set is closed (D54); `sampleFormat` moves to `@keel/testing`. RFC 0169 / ADR-0191 on `lane-r169`; RFC 0163's seams recommendation points to it.

- D68 (2026-11-21): RFC 0169 / ADR-0191 (the stdlib holds no power a library lacks) is written on `lane-r169` (c35cef19; new section k.parity). `secretPath(schema)` goes public in `@keel/schema`; `report` and `findRouter` lose their callers (std-sessions writes E2003 into its own recorder; `TableSearch` calls the public `navigate` in its own scope); `mountRequests` is the one stated exception (D54). It found more privileges than the eight imports: rules and loaders gated on the name `@keel/std-*` (`./tools`, std config, module discovery, `./routes`) become gated on the package's kind; `noEagerElement`'s std shortcut goes; E1207's four stdlib sites move under `unsafe/` (count +4, justified); S0012 and S0013 in structure-lint keep it true. Decided where the lane asked: `sampleFormat` is public in `@keel/schema`, not `@keel/testing`, since its one caller runs in the browser and a runtime package must not depend on the test harness (this corrects D67's wording); E1106 and E1207 read a library's files as a package's; E1111 trusts a schema that a package's export returns; a rule-code range for libraries goes to `tasks/FUTURE.md`. Build A (the five functions, S0012) can start after conformance; build B (the kind gates, S0013) follows RFC 0163.
- D69 (2026-11-21): the spec map's budget follows its row count. Today's RFCs add about ten sections (k.docs, k.mount-scope, k.origins, k.contract, k.routes, k.data, k.roles, k.host-element, k.host-code, k.parity, with std rows beside them), and map titles have been shortened six times, to the point where a title no longer tells an agent what the section holds. From here a merge that adds a section raises the map budget by that row's measured size and restores no title; the pre-P1 size review (backlog 0993) rewrites the titles for reading and sets the real number. Section limits stay.

- D70 (2026-11-21): RFC 0168 (`servedBy`, the closed deployable object) is pushed with ecb43f37. Decisions taken while W13 and W15 were built: (1) k.runtime counts the root's bubble listeners ("A tree using N bubbling event types has exactly N bubble listeners on its root."), since a ctx is always present under RFC 0162 and adds capture listeners the spec does not state yet (backlog 0997); (2) RFC 0159 is built in full on `lane-r159` and merges without RFC 0163's spec text (k.library, E1161 to E1164), which stays on `lane-r163` until that RFC is built, because the spec's code table makes check demand a test per code; RFC 0163 and ADR-0185 land as proposed documents; (3) `hostCode`'s `update` is optional; a `Boundary`'s fallback gets `keel.unexpected` or `schema.invalid` (k.errors); the host element count reads an app's root `modules/**` too, and under `--scope` is written and not compared; (4) the `unsafe` count rises by one, `packages/testing/unsafe/withCallbacks.ts`, justified there: `parse` keeps the keys a schema does not name, so the parsed props already hold a component's callback props; (5) check's fixture conformance must be run from `packages/check` (backlog 0996): two reports of thirteen stray E1751 failures were that and no defect.

- D71 (2026-11-21): W13's L1 (the router core) is built on `w13-l1` (router 13.0 kB, `@keel/router/contract` 3.1 kB; `bindingsOf`, `entryAt`, `allowAt` in `@keel/router/internal`; the position note is `tasks/p1/w13-l1-positions.md` there; a binding entry missing its `component`, `loader` or `action` is E1515). It showed that RFC 0157's layout binding `{ component, loader?, guard?, layout? }` drops a feature: a layout carries an `errorElement` today (`ViewDef` in the router, and k.router's "renders the nearest `errorElement` in the chain; layouts above it stay"), and 24 router conformance files bind one through a shared fixture. No feature is cut without the operator's word: a layout binding keeps `errorElement?`, and the RFC, k.contract and L1 are corrected to that; the tests that expected E1510 for it are wrong. The other conformance defects L1 found (an expected E1653 against the spec's E1503, an undefined name, two files still on the two-argument loader, a `matchHref` import from the root, misplaced `@ts-expect-error` lines) go to the conformance-writer after L1 and L2 merge into the lane.

- D72 (2026-11-21): W13 stage 2 is built (L3 server, forms and the harness's routes; L4 the compiler and module schema; L5 services and std-offline, merged), and the main line of f15c6700 is merged into `lane-r157`. What each lane leaves is in `tasks/p1/w13-l3-notes.md` and `w13-l4-notes.md`. Decided on the way: (1) the red-list rule holds for the packages a lane owns; packages downstream of it that are not moved yet (cli above all, then check) churn as upstream types change (cli went from 56 failing files to 61 with L4: 19 newly red, 14 newly green), so their lists are read at their own lane and the whole tree at stage 5; (2) after every lane merge the touched packages are type-checked on the lane: L1 and L2 were each green and together left 24 type errors in the router's tests (`mount` without a ctx); (3) a module's `public` is a closed object holding `exports` only, so a stale `routes` key is refused, as the closed deployable object of RFC 0168; (4) the generated server entry passes one route table to `start`, and the server takes what the spec states, a single table if it is silent; (5) the main line's callback-props sentence did not fit k.component in the merge and sits in k.reactive for now: it gets a proper home before the lane merges to the main line.

- D73 (2026-11-21): W13's scaffold lane (L7) is merged into `lane-r157`, and the conformance pass over the defect lists is done: router, runtime-dom, ui, forms, services and std-offline pass in full with no type error; compiler, server and testing are green but for files that wait for `keel check` and `keel build` (L6). It surfaced three points, decided here. (1) A second feature the RFC would have cut: a page may sit in a layout of another module today (the main line's `resume-app.ts` fixture binds `al.layout` around `ap.page` in one table with both modules placed), which is how an app shares one shell across feature modules; RFC 0157 says a binding's `layout` is "a layout id of the contract", and the conformance pass rewrote seven resume tests to fit. No feature is cut without the operator's word: a binding's `layout` may name a layout of another module whose contract is in the same route table, checked where the tables are joined (E1506 for an unknown id or a cycle), and the rewritten tests go back. (2) RFC 0162 says the harness passes the component "the same one its handle exposes as `ctx`", and the handle has no `ctx`: the handle of `render` and `renderRoute` exposes `ctx`, stated in k.testing. (3) k.parity's "`TableSearch` rendered with no `ctx` never navigates" is moot once every component has a ctx: the clause goes and the two-routers clause stays.

- D74 (2026-11-21): W13 stage 4. golden-2, apps/sessions and apps/inspector are rewritten and green on their branches (`w13-g2`, `w13-ap`: type-check, `keel check` with no diagnostic, tests, build), with no kernel defect found; the skills, guides, READMEs and eval tasks are rewritten on `w13-sk` (docs-check from 34 D0006 to none). golden-1 (`w13-g1`) is rewritten and stopped by a contradiction in the design: std.contracts has an app spread `...channelEntries(channel, { id })` into its contract, and the kernel reads contracts statically, so check's E1111 calls the contract unreadable and the compiler would leave the module's routes out of `routes.json`; a literal entry is refused by `channelRoute` (E1515). An opus lane on `w13-ch` settles it under three constraints: routes stay statically readable, the kernel names no stdlib function (k.parity), no channel feature is lost. After it lands: golden-1's `api.contract.ts` and binding, the skills and `docs/guide/build-golden-1.md` follow its form. Also decided: the skills lane dropped the `live` stub, the `resolve` import and the guard example from keel-data and keel-server, and a `styles=` hint from keel-client, to meet size targets; an agent needs those, so they go back and the skill size targets are re-based to the measured sizes, as package budgets are (D65). One loader serves one entry: golden-2's shared sign-in and sign-up loader became two over a helper.

- D75 (2026-11-21): the channel problem of D74 is settled on `w13-ch` (RFC 0157 D32): `channelEntries` is removed; an app writes a channel's entries out in its contract as literal entries taking their schemas from the channel, and `channelRoute(contract, channel, {...})` returns `{ loader, action }`, throwing E1515 naming a member that is not the channel's. k.contract states the static-reading rule for every package: literal keys; `method`, `path`, `layout` and `events` literal; `params`, `search` and `query` schemas followed through consts and imports; a spread or call in place of the entries or of an entry is W1005 and E1111. Evaluating contracts in `keel check` lost (the check has no module loader and must not run app code), as did a general reader for helper calls (an interpreter every reader and library would share). All four apps are green on their branches with it (golden-1 166 tests, golden-2 134, sessions 14, inspector 17; `keel check` with no diagnostic; builds ok). The skills have their dropped content back, and `docs.skill` is 2,300 tokens (keel-data 2,251, keel-server 2,248, keel-client 2,014). For stage 5: k.docs, RFC 0151 and ADR-0173 still say 2,000 per skill; `docs/guide/build-golden-1.md` names a `BoardParams` const where the app uses `Nothing`, and the guide was never run against the app; the rule's wording against E1111 still reading a spread of a const object literal.

- D76 (2026-11-21): static reading of contracts is one reader for check and compiler (`readContract` in `@keel/compiler/internal`): a spread, call, name or computed key in place of the entries or of an entry, and a non-literal `method`, `path`, `layout` or `events`, are each W1005 and E1111 at the expression, with the entries beside it read; a spread of a const object is not followed either, so every id, method and path is in the `contract(...)` call. A missing `publish` is a type error and `manifest` throws E1515. The eight kernel follow-ups of the lane are closed (E1152 with a fix and E1506 per deployable table for a cross-module `layout`; W1005 for an unresolvable placed module; the handle's `ctx` typed as the sim ctx; E1111 over an API `query` stated in k.privacy; the callback-props sentence back in k.component). golden-2, apps/sessions and apps/inspector are merged into `lane-r157`. A whole-tree run on the lane before the apps merged: 12,526 tests, 48 failing in 46 files, 42 of them waiting for apps or skills; no failure was load-related (the two cli files once called so fail alone). Left for the conformance-writer, listed in `tasks/p1/w13-status.md` on the lane: 53 `@ts-expect-error` sites in 20 conformance files that the structure lint counts as suppressions (the lint stays; negative type cases go through a helper under `unsafe/`, as the unit tests now do), three cli files assuming `routes.ts` reaches the browser or placing `contract()` in `routes.ts`, std-messages' two files, the E1514 fixture's non-literal path.

- D77 (2026-11-21): the review and the red-team pass on `lane-r157` (both: merge after fixes; no guard bypass, the order 405, 404, 403, 422 leaks nothing a per-method probe does not learn; a foreign layout's guard and loader run before the page's). Decided, one fix lane on the lane: (1) check maps a TypeScript error to E1510, E1506 or E1515 only when it sits on a member's key or on a value that is a literal or a name; an error inside an inline component, guard, loader, action or title body, or inside a schema expression, stays E1751 with its own text; an error under an unprefixed key is not dropped with the key's E1155. (2) `contract` reached through an alias (`const mk = contract`) is refused by the placement rule, so no contract escapes the static reader; `contract` also throws at run time for a key without its module's prefix. (3) An action's returned `Href` cannot leave the origin: `href` refuses an empty param segment, `isHref` refuses `//` and a backslash, and the redirect answer checks again (found: `ok(ap.href('ap.open', { a: '', b: 'evil.example' }))` answered 303 to `//evil.example`; older than this RFC). (4) The identity mount, reversing part of D60: no page matches a path that decodes to the mount or under it, so `/%61pi/auth` is 404 and no longer a page; the handler's own test stays textual. (5) A service client refuses `.` and `..` as a path param and encodes every param, and caps the bytes it reads from a response. (6) A request for a stream on an entry that is not live answers 406 after the guards, not before, so the answer does not tell which guarded entries are live. (7) RFC 0157 names what it removes: one loader bound to several routes. Left as stated: a 405's `allow` lists the methods bound at the path. Backlog 1000: `routes.json` writes `guard: null` both for no guard and for a guard without `redirect`. The `unsafe` count is 18: the services lane deleted `asClient.ts`, and the conformance helper files hold no `unsafe(` call.

- D78 (2026-11-21): the D77 fixes are applied on `lane-r157` with conformance (testing 548, router 244, check 1,552, services 92, server 914, ui 232 conformance tests passing; all four apps' `keel check` and tests green). Following the open redirect to its end found more than the document POST: a client `Form` submit whose action returned a bad link called `location.assign` with it (seen with `//evil.example/x`); the first fix answered 500 after the action's stores were saved; `navigate` given a raw string passed a backslash or a scheme on. Now, stated once in k.router Links: `Link to`, `navigate`, `Form action` and an action's `ok` take an `Href` only, rechecked when run on server and client; a bad one saves nothing and follows nothing (500 `keel.unexpected` on both transports, the Form's error slot in the client, E1501 from `navigate` and `Form`, `about:blank` from `Link`). Limits stated by the lane: `navigate('/a/\x')` stays a same-origin document navigation (a k.mount-scope conformance case); a plain `<a href>` written by app code is checked by the URL policy alone. A service client refuses `''`, `.` and `..` as a param, encodes `/` and a backslash, reads at most `maxBytes` (1 MiB by default) and follows no redirect. Left open: stream events have no size cap of their own (backlog 1001). Running: a sonnet agent closes the text items in `.worktrees/r157` (ADR-0052 superseded, statuses, 2,300 per skill in k.docs, the guide against the app, three deleted sentences checked, the `w13-*` working files removed). Then the gate on the lane's head, then the squash.

The gate ran on the lane's head f6342c6b, whose tree is the main line's but for plan files: build, both apps' `keel test`, the ledger and evidence written there (1,729 items, 1,524 verified, 18 unsafe, 231 stale mutation claims), `pnpm check`, `pnpm ledger --diff` with 0 lowered and 0 uncovered, the full `pnpm test` of 85 tasks with no failing file; then build, check and the ledger diff once more on the pushed head. W13's gate in `tasks/p1/PLAN.md` is met: no endpoint is declared outside a contract in either golden app, and E1561 is gone from the check. `lane-r157` is kept as a branch until CI is green on the push.
  What the build decided beyond the RFC as first written, for the operator to confirm or reverse: a layout keeps `errorElement` and may belong to another module (D71, D73); a channel's entries are written out in the contract and no spread or call stands in for an entry (D75, D76); `sessions.tally` is always served where std-sessions is placed, behind its reader guard (D73's lane); one loader serves one entry; the identity mount is tested on the decoded path (D77); a link is same-origin wherever it is followed (D78); the skill size is 2,300 tokens (D74).

- D80 (2026-11-21): CI was red from the push of RFC 0161 on, and it went unseen for hours: each push cancelled the run before it, and the first run to finish was on a plan-only push that was not looked at. The failure was one std-capacitor conformance test, right on macOS and wrong on the Linux runner: its "no `java`" environment kept `/bin` on `PATH`, which on the runner is a link to `/usr/bin`, where a system `java` sits. The product was right (it reads `$JAVA_HOME/bin/java` or `java` on `PATH`, nothing else); the test's environment now holds only its own stand-in directory (0cb7e06b). The Method gains a step: after a push that carries code, the CI run on that head is watched to its end before the next push that would cancel it, and plan-only commits ride with the next code push. A local gate on macOS does not stand in for the Linux runner.

RFC 0236 (ADR-0258, `lane-s09` b545d4f2), personal data: a recording holds what a schema encodes, so no undeclared key; parsed values are plain data; `perAnonymous` counted process-wide.
- D163 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): every property test in the repo runs from a fixed seed. CI run 45591293688 failed on a household property written the same day with no seed, which the gate had passed three times; an unseeded property is a gate that flakes and a failure nobody can replay. `lane-ciseed` (`.worktrees/cifix`) finds the counterexample and its cause, seeds every property after trying each under twenty seeds, adds a repo lint, proposes the app-facing rule, and makes the reporter print a failed property's seed, path and counterexample (CI printed `undefined`).
- D164 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; corrects D163's account): the household property that failed CI was seeded (4242, through a shared constant) and held under twenty other seeds; it timed out. It renders fifty pages in about 1 s here, and CI's runner is about 8.5 times slower, past vitest's 5 s default. The orchestrator misread the test's call as unseeded from a cut excerpt. What stands: `tooling/property-times.ts` gives a fast-check `assert` 300 ms per run as it already gave `properties` and `simulate`; a failed test's `fix` line carries the line its clipped message cut, and a property's seed, path and counterexample; structure-lint S0015 refuses an unseeded property (three real ones were found in package and tooling tests and seeded after passing twenty seeds each; three more in `packages/ctx/conformance` are seeded by the conformance-writer, after which S0015's skip of conformance folders goes). The gate's slow run did not reach CI's slowness: backlog 1115; until it is fixed the gate runs its burner step with twice the core count (32 here).
- D165 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): RFC 0236's calls. A stream whose request has a `user` counts against that user (`perCaller`), never the anonymous count: a signed-in person is not shut out by an anonymous flood. Parse costs about a third more than before (0.53 to 0.71 µs for a five-field object; one descriptor read per key is what tells a data property from an accessor without running a getter), accepted. The marked values of a store's state are kept as last given, by `initial`, a restore, a commit or an undo, and every recorded error is scrubbed of them, object keys included.
- D166 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): for a package of kind `library` the check imports only its `./tools` export and never its component entry; a library with no `./tools` needs no build for an app's check, dev, test or build. Slice 3's builder had documented the opposite (an unbuilt linked library is E1159) and added jsx-runtime exports that throw so a built entry could be imported. The stdlib packages that register from their entry move that into `./tools` in slice 4. A library declares the kernel as peers (five: compiler, reactive, runtime-dom, schema, ui). In slice 4b, a registering export used as a value check cannot follow is E1607 at the use, and a library's bundled file is copied wherever the library's real folder is.
- D167 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): the gate measures slowness by arithmetic, not by burners. `KEEL_TEST_TIME_SCALE` multiplies every test time bound the repo sets; a suite that passes here at 0.1 passes on a runner ten times slower. Three pushes in a row were green in the gate and red in CI, twice for a test that fit its bound here and not on CI's runner (8.5 times slower). `lane-slowci` builds it, sweeps every package and app at 0.1 and fixes what fails at its cause, with a lint against a hard-coded test timeout that goes around it.
- D168 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): `lane-lib` merged into the main line (18eaa98f) with slices 1, 2a, 2b, 3 and 4b, once check's `explain-and-fixture-per-code` case passed with an empty list (D156's condition): `@keel/check` `ok 3213 tests`. Slices 4 (the stdlib on the format) and 5 (k.parity's second part) are ordinary lanes off the main line. The ledger's `hostElements` rises from 0 to 1 with `examples/library-charts`' `Host` chart, which is the example's purpose; the next regeneration accepts it and says so.

The outside accessibility audit and penetration test run after P1 or at its end and reopen the stage only if they find cause; they are not raised with the operator before then, nor are the npm and GitHub organizations, which wait for the end of P1. - D173 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): E1106 (`s.format` in app code) does not apply to a library's own files: defining format types is what a library such as std-text, std-temporal, std-messages or std-tokens is for, and an app still may not. It goes in as an amendment to RFC 0163 with spec text and conformance before any mover relies on it; the stdlib's formats lane waits on it.
- D174 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): no library exemption for E1105 and E1107 (secrets reaching client code) or E1576 (another package's `internal` entry): a library is held to the app's rule. The code moves into `*.server.ts`, and what workflows and schedules need from another package is exported from that package's public entry. E1513 in std-health is read at its site before anything is decided; E1207 stays with slice 5.
- D175 (2026-11-25, orchestrator): `lane-std-pilot` changed rules to pass (S0014 in a library's package, the own-folder reading dropping folders outside the listed five, four dropped test assertions): it merges only after a reviewer's independent read of those changes, and with its fixes.
- D176 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): E1207's four stdlib sites (`scope({ detached })` in statecharts, conflicts, offline) move into `unsafe/` in the mover lanes, not in slice 5: an app that lists the moved library reports the site and cannot go green otherwise.
- D177 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): a time bound is stated from the work a test does and is not raised to fit this machine under load; the gate's run at `KEEL_TEST_TIME_SCALE=0.1` is trusted only when nothing else heavy runs, and a cli case that fails at 0.1 only under load is not a failure to fix.
- D178 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): E1607's capability naming (`<id>.<lowerCamel>`) holds for a library; std-tokens' and std-geo's capabilities `tokens` and `geo` are renamed to that form in their specs, packages and the apps that list them (nothing is released, so they are rewritten directly), the names chosen from what each capability grants. One naming rule, no exception for a library whose capability is its id.
- D179 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): a scaffolded project's first `keel test` passes: the workspace scaffold writes a first test, since k.test-run ends a project with a config and no test file as T0001. And k.library-pack's own-folder list stands over k.formats #10: the own-folder reading does not read a library folder's `modules/`, so #10 drops that assertion.
- D180 (2026-11-25, operator): an app approves a listed library's capabilities. Where the app lists a library it names the capabilities it allows it; a library holding one the app did not grant is an error at check; this holds for every capability, `observe` and `net` among them. RFC 0240's `moduleStores()` behind `observe` stands with that grant built. A kernel read that returns state already redacted by schema, so no library holds raw values, is recorded as a later strengthening (a backlog line), not built now.
- D181 (2026-11-25, orchestrator; to confirm or reverse): a bound that is plainly too tight for its case's work is restated from the work; the whole gate runs only with nothing else running on the machine; a failure on yet another bound after that calls for one allowance rule across packages, not more single fixes. D177's claim that cli failed at 0.1 only under load was wrong for three cases (checkpoint 98).

## Method
- RFC unit: RFC, spec and ADR; then conformance by the `conformance-writer` from the spec; then the build. Conformance and build run in their own git worktree and branch, merged when green, so red tests never sit on the main line.
- A lane that changes `@keel/check`, `@keel/compiler` or `@keel/schema` runs the cli suite too (D33).
- After a push that carries code, watch the CI run on that head to its end before pushing again (a push cancels the run in progress); plan-only commits wait for the next code push (D80).
- Before a push that carries code, after `pnpm build`: the smokes CI runs (`tooling/smoke/restart.mjs`, `workflow.mjs`, `dev-signin.mjs`, `resume.mjs 3`), since no package suite serves a built app in a browser (backlog 1003).
- Before a push: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, both apps' `keel test`, `pnpm ledger --diff`, `node tooling/evidence-report.mjs --check` and `pnpm build` on the exact commit in a clean checkout, each gated on its own exit status. `pnpm ledger` and `pnpm evidence:report` are written from a clean tree after both apps' `keel test`.
- The gate's root `pnpm test` counts only when its own last line is `ok` with every task run. A failure, flake or not, is fixed or rerun and then the whole root `pnpm test` is run again: a file passing alone says nothing about the tasks the failed run did not finish (D97; before 93cc1ac9 turbo cancelled the running tasks at the first failure and the summary showed one failure).
- The committed ledger is written only from a tree where `keel test` has just run in exactly the apps CI's check job tests (golden-1, golden-2, household, inspector, sessions, each `examples/*`: every project with a `.keel/routes.json`, since each carries a11y items, D159): a `pnpm ledger` in a worktree without those reports drops the a11y rows, and one with extra reports adds rows CI reads as lowered. A lane's subagent regenerates the ledger only after running those `keel test`s there; the gate's `ledger-fresh --a11y` in the clean checkout is the judge (D100).
- One mutation run at a time (machine-wide lock). Few test-running agents at once: the machine is shared, and spawn-heavy cli conformance times out under load (backlog 0938).

## Lanes (2026-11-19)
Each lane is a subagent in a worktree under `.worktrees/` (created by the orchestrator from the current head with `git worktree add` and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline`; the Agent tool's own isolation starts from `master` and is not used), committing on its branch; the orchestrator reviews, cherry-picks, and removes the worktree. Security lanes get a red-team pass before the merge; a red-team brief says findings go in the report, not the backlog.
- `.worktrees/w03`, branch `lane-w03`: the return-link design of D18. The spec rewrite of RFC 0150 is committed there (915ded3e: RFC 0150 and ADR-0172 renamed `links-into-a-shell`, a new section `k.links`, `k.return`, `k.opening`, `k.mount` at exactly its 6,000-character limit, the threat model). Its shape: `identityMount('/api/auth', { links: ['/magic-link/verify', '/verify-email'] })` declares the mount paths any link may open, checked by E1584; the shell reports its launch once (links, none, or a failed read) and an opening made with no person's act is held until then. Next on that branch: conformance by the `conformance-writer` from the RFC's Conformance diff (three bullets move word for word from `k.return` to `k.links`, two reverse, three are removed, nine are added; existing `return-*` files change under `rfc:0150`), then the build (the `wip:` commit 1334ca3c holds act detection to reuse and puts `packages/testing` 378 bytes over its 13.5 kB budget; the READMEs, the keel-testing skill, changesets and an E1584 explanation are the build lane's), a red-team pass, the merge, then the ledger and evidence.   - The operator decided on 2026-11-22 (D102): RFC 0186 yes, in the kernel; RFC 0188 yes, all of it, a lone `gap` is a check error (E1809); RFC 0193 yes as proposed; the three gate-tooling changes yes. And a principle (D103): identity is opt-in. A front end with no back end (no identity files, no `node` deployable) is a first-class app: it makes no request to a Keel server path, carries no config and ideally no bytes for sign-in; a half-opted-in app (identity files, no deployable to answer) is a check error, not a silent run-time failure; an app that opts in gets all of Keel's architecture and protection, no lighter path, and the proofs that enforce them (pii canaries, compliance rules, evidence) cover the new surface.

- Checkpoint 94 of 2026-11-25: the reviewer on RFC 0240: merge after fixes, and one point is the operator's. (1) E1156's gate is bypassable: `scanFile.ts` sees only an import declaration from `@keel/store` or a namespace member; `export { moduleStores } from '@keel/store'`, `export * from`, `await import('@keel/store')`, `const { moduleStores } = ns` and `require` are silent (the same shape of hole as RFC 0238's makers): a fixer closes it once the conformance-writer leaves the lane. (2) **An app cannot see or refuse `observe` in a library it lists**: E1156 compares a file with its own module's manifest; nothing surfaces a listed library's `capabilities` to the app (check output, ledger, evidence); `observe` already grants every nav, input, error and net event, and RFC 0240 extends it to a raw read of every module store; a third-party library with `observe` and `net` has that power with no redaction. (3) The read-tap change is sound, both directions tested, no resumability regression found. (4) E1107's skip keys on the binding. The reviewer's view: a kernel read returning schema-redacted state would be the stronger protection; the chosen design is acceptable only with (2) closed. ASKED OF THE OPERATOR (not decided): how an app sees and approves a listed library's capabilities; the orchestrator recommends an app-side grant (the app names, where it lists the library, the capabilities it allows it; a library holding one not granted is an error at check), for every capability and not `observe` alone, with the redacted read recorded as a later strengthening. `lane-std-sessions` does not merge until answered.

- Checkpoint 142 of 2026-11-26: household's simulation failure reproduced on Linux (a `node:22` container at 1 CPU with two busy loops: `E1728 ... Timeout`, the stalled resource a 10,000 ms `setTimeout` from PGlite's `__setitimer_js`), fixed, merged (206d358d; `lane-ci-sim` removed) and pushed. Mechanism, demonstrated: Postgres in PGlite arms a 10 s wall-clock statistics timer in whatever async context runs the statement; the household server's ctx is not driven by the run, so its statements ran in the run's context and the timer became the run's work; on a machine slow enough for a test to outlive the timer armed at setup, the drive waits each timer out in real time until the budget is gone: real I/O inside simulated time, not a tight bound. Fix: PGlite calls run in an `AsyncResource` of their own (a test fails without it). Also: a budget spent mid-shrink now throws the earlier real failure, labelled `shrunk until the budget ran out`, where E1728 used to replace it. Linux 5 of 5 at the failing setting; household 188, testing 1,082, std-postgres 208 green; `pnpm check --quiet` green. Budgets: std-postgres/server 8.4kb, /testing 1.65kb. Noted: CI's shards run at time scale 1 (its log sets no `KEEL_TEST_TIME_SCALE`); the gate's 0.1 stands in for a slow runner but did not find this one, which needed a starved CPU: the slow-machine gate job of checkpoint 141 is the way. Owed, not blocking: a spec sentence for k.drive Stalls ("A budget spent while a failing run is shrunk ends the shrinking: the smallest failing run so far is thrown with its own error, `shrunk until the budget ran out`") with conformance, by the conformance-writer under an RFC; the same exposure in `apps/household/test/reconnect.sim.test.ts:20` is covered by the fix.

- D191 (2026-11-26, operator): the orchestrator is the project's director of engineering and PM, and its first goal is the project done right, not done. Neither it nor a subagent weakens Keel to get past something: less `unsafe`, not more; higher test and mutation scores, not lower; the right design and the best public surface, not a workaround; no exemption, loosened assertion, raised limit or skipped check as a shortcut, and a subagent's shortcut is sent back. It may have a subagent review other subagents' sessions for common failure patterns and fix them in the briefs, the skills or the project, and may write new skills and specialised agents. Updates between events are one or two sentences; questions are held until the operator writes; a status answer is the big picture of what is left to public (what is broken, what needs designing and building, what needs testing or stricter testing, what has not been run, which doc sweeps and sites are owed), not what agents are doing.
