- Checkpoint 148 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0242 and ADR-0264 written with spec text in `lane-r-defect` (3a4d7b70; check fails only `ledger-fresh`). The design: `defect(message, code?): never` and `isDefect` in `@keel/schema` beside `ok`, `err` and `attempt`; a defect goes where any throw goes (the nearest Boundary as `keel.unexpected`, the server's answer and scrubbed log, a failed test or simulation); `attempt(fn, expect)` turns only what `expect` recognises into an err and rethrows the rest, so a bug cannot be turned back into a value; `try`/`finally` without `catch` is cleanup; an `unsafe(` range no longer lifts E1451 or E1452 and a bare `throw` stays refused everywhere; E1455 for a defect code that is not a literal; review rule R1013 flags a defect on a `Result`'s err branch (the abuse guard); a module root is a scope made while no scope runs, which the runtime already does, so a library's module-level state lives in one and E1207 is judged in every file but a kernel package's own. The settle helpers become `attempt` with `expect`. Count: main 33, the formats lane 41, after 14 on the main line (15 with the formats lane): what stays are 14 type casts over values the code built and reads of the DOM or clock no ctx capability covers. D195 (the writer's recommendations, accepted by the orchestrator under D191, to confirm or reverse): apps too lose `throw` and `catch` inside `unsafe(`; no library detaches a scope (this reverses RFC 0238's lift); the defect code is optional for now and the kernel's own throws are a later sweep. Backlog from it: E1452 misses `.then(onOk, onRejected)` (std-offline's `flush.ts`); the shells' net drivers throw `TypeError` by contract. The conformance-writer runs there now; then a builder migrates eleven stdlib packages, then the two std lanes.

The sweep found 57 real floating promises and real bugs behind some: a rejected `Form` submit never reached a Boundary; a failed shell-form request left the form pending for ever; a live stream's iterator cleanup discarded its throw; an evicted PGlite folder's failed close floated; a failed launch report in testing was lost; six app handlers dropped their rejections. Stopped: (1) long-lived browser loops with no public owner (std-offline `drain` and `flush`, std-live, std-channels `subscribe`, std-tables `TableSearch`, std-capacitor's net, and apps calling `drain`); the builder's option of a kernel-internal reporter by allowlist is refused (no private door for the stdlib); (2) callback types in std-headless and std-tables and `Part`'s `onKey` that cannot carry a promise to the runtime, which blocks app handlers in golden-2 and household; so golden-1, golden-2 and household fail `keel check` in the lane and six cli cases with them. The RFC's writer is resumed to design the public owner (structured concurrency: work owned by a scope, cancelled on dispose, its rejection to that scope's Boundary or module root; `resource` or one small primitive) and the callback rule for component libraries, and to settle two disputes (`Part` slider and radio handlers pinned `=> void` in ui's conformance; the exact handler type). To review before this lane merges: sweep fixes that may silence instead of own (`.then(undefined, forget)` on cache watchers; household's `queueMicrotask`). Measured there and owed to the backlog: `keel check` warm on household is 2.5 to 2.8 s against a stated budget of under 1 s, missed before this change.

- Checkpoint 160 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0245's second part is written (`lane-server-up` 9d8079f8d), accepted. D197 (the writer's design, accepted by the orchestrator, to confirm or reverse: it adds a kernel primitive): `task(ctx, work: (signal) => Promise<unknown>): void` in `@keel/reactive` is the one public owner of browser work with a lifetime: owned by the running scope (E1203 outside one), cancelled by an `AbortSignal` when the scope is disposed, its rejection to the nearest `onError` or Boundary and else to `ctx` as an `error` event, never to the process; a server render does not run it; `simulate` tracks it and names it in E1728; a test's end aborts it. `resource` stays the owner of a value. The eight sites are rewritten in the RFC (`drain` becomes `void` and starts tasks in the caller's scope; `flush`, `live`, `subscribe`, `TableSearch`, the capacitor net). Callbacks: `Handler<A = []> = ((...a: A) => void) | ((...a: A) => Promise<void>)` from `@keel/runtime-dom` for every callback the kernel owns; `Part`'s `onKey` returns `boolean | Promise<void>`; a library types its callback props `Handler` and returns their result; E1456 at an app's async argument to a `=> void` prop and at a library statement that drops a `Handler`'s result; std-headless and std-tables move to it; `roles-native-controls.test.ts:137-139` pinned more than the spec says and is reversed. No new codes; glossary gains "task". The RFC no longer claims a warm-check budget it does not meet. Backlog from it: `.then(f, g)` with two arguments handles a rejection E1452 does not see; the builder confirms `ctx.net` cancels an aborted response's body. Its conformance-writer runs; then the builder finishes the sweep, and a reviewer reads the sweep's fixes for silencing.

- Checkpoint 174 of 2026-11-26: the operator's notes after hearing the podcast, checked against the tree by three readers, and what follows from each. (1) A browser tool over the queryable runtime: today there is a dev-only `GET /__keel/inspect` (stores and their logs, the dependency graph, ids, the a11y tree, routes), `keel inspect`, `keel mcp` and `apps/inspector`; nothing reads client state from the page and there is no extension; production answers 404 by design. Planned as RFC 0250, after the freeze (it rides the surface the open RFCs are still changing): one WebExtension for Chrome and Firefox with panels for stores and their transaction logs, the live dependency graph, scopes and tasks, the a11y tree with an overlay, ids, tokens and contrast per element, the session recorder (record, replay, export a test), and a server panel from the dev server (requests, loaders and actions, jobs, workflows, schedules, health, log records). (2) `keel check` after an edit reports E1005 until `keel build` runs: check knows the artifact is stale, so it refreshes the derived `.keel/` files itself before judging; a stale committed artifact stays an error only in a frozen mode for CI. Promoted from W17's paper cuts to its own lane. (3) Session recordings, D199 (operator: fix it): the ingest route `POST /sessions` has no token or signature, only a same-site check and a size cap; reading goes through `GET /sessions/list` behind a guard over caller hashes listed in the app, and the nightly job goes through the app with a token. Redesign as RFC 0248: the endpoint is write-only as far as the framework goes (no list route, no reader guard, no viewer registration); an upload needs a short-lived signed grant the server mints with the page (bound to origin, expiry, size and count, in the manner of a presigned upload), so the endpoint is never open; the server writes to a configured sink (a folder, an object store, Postgres) it never reads back; `keel session` pulls from that storage or reads a local folder and replays, minimises and writes tests there; the nightly action reads the storage with the operator's own credentials; a static app with no server points the recorder at a sink URL its own back end signs. Securing the storage is the operator's. (4) Themes, D200 (operator): light and dark, named theme choices and print against screen are all first class and cheap to switch. Today tokens compile to CSS custom properties on `:root` and each style prop to one atomic class that reads a property (`p="2"` is `.k-p-2{padding:var(--k-space-2)}`), so a theme is another set of values for the same properties under one selector and switching it is one attribute on `<html>`, with no class or render change. RFC 0243 (in build) has two schemes only and nothing for print. RFC 0249 generalises it: a theme is a named partial token set with a condition (a media query such as the colour preference, contrast preference or print, and or the visitor's choice), contrast judged per theme; a `print` condition for style props beside breakpoints; every opaque colour form; an importer from design tokens (`keel tokens import` for the W3C design tokens file Figma variables export, modes as themes). RFC numbers: 0247 `pnpm create keel`, 0248 sessions, 0249 themes, 0250 the browser tool. All seven slots are busy with builders; these start as slots free, 0248 first.

- Checkpoint 176 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0248 and ADR-0276 written with spec text in `lane-r-sessions` (8399a192a, based on `lane-std-sessions`), accepted. The signed upload is a ticket (the glossary holds "grant" already) and a kernel feature: a write entry declares `ticket: { uses, bytes, ttl }`; a node deployable puts `<meta name="keel-ticket">` in every document it serves; a ticket is `<expiry>.<uses>.<bytes>.<nonce>.<mac>`, an HMAC under a key derived from `KEEL_SECRET` over origin, deployable, entry, expiry, uses, bytes and nonce; spent uses in `ctx.storage`; anything else is 403 and the handler never runs; `POST /__keel/ticket` renews one that expired up to 24 h ago. `sessions.ingest` is the package's only entry (2 uses, 256 KiB, 1 h), answers `{}` or an error code, and writes to the capability `sessions.archive` (write, no read) chosen by `KEEL_SESSIONS_ARCHIVE`: `file:` (refuses when full), `s3://`, `postgres:`, `https:` as the plug-in point. A static app sends to its own sink (E2011, E2012). What a ticket proves, stated in the RFC: a server holding the secret served a page on this origin within the hour; not a browser, a person or true content. Codes E1516, E2011, E2012, E2013; new section std.sessions-ingest (13 clauses). Accepted with it: tickets in the kernel; no TypeScript archive plug-in; a full folder archive refuses; tally deltas may lose ten minutes on a crash; a client deployable hosted apart from its node uses its own sink. Its conformance-writer runs; then a builder, then a breaking reviewer (the ticket is new security surface).

- Checkpoint 177 of 2026-11-26: two breaking reviews. (A) `lane-server-up` (RFC 0245): merge after fixes. Major: on a server a `task`'s rejection goes to `ctx`, which nothing there observes, so it vanishes with no E1577, no health count and a scope alive per request: the simplest way round the RFC; `.then(() => {}, undefined)` and an array of promises from `xs.map(async …)` pass E1456; the fix `await (save(), 1)` awaits `1`; in the browser an error with no `onError` and no observer now prints nothing where a dropped promise used to reach `unhandledrejection` and monitoring tools, and a handler's rejection after unmount is dropped. Minor: a reason whose `stack` getter throws or a revoked Proxy turns one rejection into an exit; `addEventListener` with an async listener and object-method shorthand are missed; a stored, never-read promise passes (two conformance fixtures use that); a throwing `onError` reaches the process; the health endpoint's meaning as liveness or readiness is unstated. Held: the count and the 503 at exactly the 100th through 41,000 rejections, no thrown text in the log, memory level, the health route's headers, the fix's placement, task disposal, the sweep's kept sites. The RFC's writer is resumed to close all eight in the spec (what a task is on a server; no error ends with no observer and no report, as an invariant). (B) `lane-std-sessions` (RFC 0240, both parts): **do not merge**, three blockers, each with check green: the `./tools` approval digest pins one file while a self-import by package name, a `#` import, a computed `import()` or a read-and-`new Function` runs unapproved code; a library's code outside `src/` and `unsafe/` escapes every rule, ships in the bundle and ran at build; a nested second version of a library is invisible to check, the grants and the approvals. Major: a non-literal library manifest is not judged. Minor: `--approve` approves everything at once, non-interactively; the recorded rule codes pin nothing; a ledger can claim items that do not exist. Held: no marker file without a grant and approval; rules inside a library's `src/` hold even when `unsafe` is approved. D202 (the orchestrator's): the seven holes of three reviews (these, RFC 0243's entry conditions, E1170, E1171) are one design fault: check starts from the files it expects and the bundler from what resolves. No seventh patch: RFC 0251 (`lane-r-boundary`, the one fable subagent, on `lane-std-sessions`) designs the trust boundary: the judged set derived from the same resolution the build, dev server and test runner use; every reached file classified (app, Keel library by real folder and version, plain package, built-in) and judged or explicitly fenced; everything evaluated on the developer's machine listed, read as data where it can be, approved by a digest over the whole package folder where it must run; literal-only manifests; a build that fails if the bundler loads a file check did not classify; and what a plain npm package is held to, said honestly. It reconciles RFC 0239, 0240, 0242, 0243 and 0245 and says what makes `lane-std-sessions` mergeable.

- Checkpoint 178 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0245's eight review holes are closed in the spec (`lane-server-up` cff000607), accepted. A task does not exist on a server: E1209 at a `task` call in a server-only file or one only server files import, and a throw before `work` at run time (a server already has three owners: the request's awaited promise, `enqueue` and runners). `then`'s second argument counts only as a function; an array of promises left as a statement floats; E1456 judges whether a promise floats and E1452 whether a catch is allowed, so a swallowing `.catch` is one E1452 and no E1456; inside an `await` operand the comma and `void` operands are judged and a fix is offered only where it awaits the promise. k.ctx Reports: no error ends unseen: one no `onError` or Boundary takes, a late one, or one a throwing `onError` raises is a ctx `error` event and, with no observer, goes to `reportError`; only a task's own cancellation is dropped. The process listeners never throw. A stored, never-read promise or `Result` is reported at its binding (E1456, E1453), closing the blind spot two fixtures used. `/__keel/health` is a liveness signal only an exit clears. One code, E1209. To make RFC 0242 agree (queued for its writer): `void p.catch(g)` is one E1452 and no E1456; its `.then(f, g)` counts only when `g` is a function. Its conformance-writer runs; then its builder.

- Checkpoint 181 of 2026-11-26: the operator asked what Keel has for animation and reduced motion. Read from the tree: a `motion` token group of named durations (`brandTokens` holds `fast: 120ms`) emitted as custom properties, each set to `0ms` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`; nothing consumes them: no transition or animation style prop, no keyframes, no enter or exit, no view transitions, no way for code to read the preference. D203 (operator: support the most modern sites; the design is the orchestrator's proposal): RFC 0252, motion, written after RFC 0249 since both extend the token grammar of k.values: (1) transitions as style props over named durations and named easings (an `ease` group), judged like any token; (2) named keyframes in tokens (a positive grammar, compiled once, namespaced for a library) with enter and exit on mount and unmount, the element kept until its exit ends; (3) page and shared-element transitions through the router on the View Transitions API, progressive where unsupported; (4) scroll-driven and in-view animations by named timeline; (5) reduced motion by construction: durations already go to zero; a keyframe animation states its reduced form or does not run; `keel check` refuses an endless animation with no pause control and warns on animating layout properties; (6) code that animates (canvas, the Web Animations API, springs) reads `ctx`'s reduced-motion signal and runs through a scope-owned call, not a raw `element.animate`; (7) themes may change motion. RFC 0245's review conformance is written (`lane-server-up` 4118c4484: 45 new cases over reactive, runtime-dom, server and check) and its builder is resumed.

- D205 (2026-11-26, operator): a design mode in `keel dev`, a tool no other framework has. A designer changes token values live and sees the result across the app, on one page or on one component; contrast is judged as they edit, by the same judge `keel check` uses; the work covers a new theme or a whole redesign as well as styling pages and components an agent built structurally; the result is exported as a change to `tokens.ts` that becomes a pull request. It rests on what already holds: tokens are custom properties, so a live edit is one property set and no re-render. It is RFC 0253, written after RFC 0249 (themes and `keel tokens import`) and sharing a surface with RFC 0250 (the browser tool): the designer decides whether it is a panel of that extension, an overlay the dev server serves, or both. In scope before v1: the live editor, scoping a preview to a page or component, live contrast and the a11y judgement, creating and switching themes, the export as a patch and the command that applies it. After v1, recorded in `tasks/FUTURE.md`: generating a file a design tool imports (Figma and others), so a design can start from the app's tokens.

- Checkpoint 189 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0248's review findings are closed in the spec (`lane-r-sessions` 5f65a8ac8; RFC §7), accepted. A ticketed request goes through seven ordered steps and spends a use only after its body is within `bytes` and decodes; junk counts as refusals (20 in 60 s answer 503 to that source only); a source holds at most 64 ledger entries, and past 65,536 in total only sources holding fewer than 4 get in, so filling the ledger takes 1,024 sources; entries hold a keyed hash of the source. `ticket.hour: { uses, bytes, total }` is required: the ingest allows 8 uploads and 2 MiB per source and 256 MiB for all sources an hour; one fingerprint is stored 5 times a day; the folder archive evicts its own files oldest first, never one under an hour old. Renewal counts only a verified ticket, each ticket renews once, a chain ends 7 days after its `born`. Nesting past 64 is 422. Keel no longer creates the Postgres table: the operator creates it with insert only and the server checks that at start; files 0600; `s3` over `http:` only on loopback; E2013 before listening. One rule for a request's source, which also fixes a kernel fault: the socket's peer address, or with `KEEL_PROXIES` n the n-th `x-forwarded-for` entry from the end (IPv6 by /64; W1595 when the header arrives at 0); until now the kernel trusted the last entry, which a client writes, for the CSP report limit too. std.contracts follows k.services (`api.status` 403). Stated residue: anyone who can load pages can store about 2 MiB per source and 512 MiB per storage an hour; a rate limit in front is required. `lane-r-sessions`' disputes are judged (b28a938f6: 385 of 386 std-sessions cases, tickets 25, E1516 9, compose 3). Its review conformance is being written; then its builder. RFC 0252's writer runs (`lane-r-motion`, on `lane-r-themes`). The conformance-writer judges RFC 0245's last disputes. Queued for a slot: RFC 0249's conformance.

- Checkpoint 191 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0252 and ADR-0288 are written with spec text in `lane-r-motion` (39ae38502, 216ade7d1; on `lane-r-themes`), accepted. Tokens say how things move: `motion` durations (a value or `{ full, reduced }`), `ease`, `opacity`, `shift`, `scale`, `rotate`, and `keyframes` whose frames name tokens only and whose reduced form never moves; `view` turns on page and shared-element transitions. Style props `opacity`, `shift`, `scale`, `rotate` work with states and breakpoints; `transition={{ props, duration, ease }}`; `animate` with `paused` or a `scroll` or `view` timeline; `enter` and `exit`; `share` names a shared element. Compositor and paint props may transition; layout props and height are refused (a type error and E1827). Exit: the content's scope is disposed at once as today, and each root element with `exit` stays in place, inert, without its id, until its animation ends, so focus, events, the a11y tree and `find` treat it as gone; in the harness and under `simulate` enter is skipped and exit removes in the same commit, so no test sleeps. Reduced motion re-values custom properties under the media feature or `data-k-motion="reduce"`, as a scheme does; the visitor's choice is `appearance(ctx).set({ motion: "reduce" })` and there is no `full` (an app cannot turn on motion the platform turned off). `keel check`: E1828 an animation over 5 s with no reactive `paused` (WCAG 2.2.2); E1829 a moving transition whose duration has a non-zero `reduced` (2.3.3); E1830 a token flash over 3 a second (2.3.1, stated as unable to judge area, video, canvas or code); E1831 one `share` value twice on a page; contrast judged at an element's resting opacity. No new section (the maps are full): clauses in k.values, k.compiler, k.aria, k.runtime, k.head, k.ctx, k.host-code. Its conformance waits for RFC 0249 and RFC 0245 to merge. Owed: motion values in `brandTokens` (brand). The conformance-writer works on `lane-r-boundary`'s shell fixtures and six disputes.

- Checkpoint 194 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0243's second review is settled in the spec (`lane-r-look` a795c52f6; RFC decisions D16 to D23), accepted. k.values: outside quotes a value holds only named ASCII; a quoted string holds no backslash, control character or lone surrogate; literal numbers carry no sign but a shadow's offsets and spread, so a tokenizer reads each form one way; widened with `calc`, `min`, `max`, `clamp` (nested at most four deep, 32 names, type rules), `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, every current unit, commas in quoted families, `currentColor` in `border` and `shadow`; contrast judges a `clamp()` font size at its first argument; still refused: `url()`, a foreign `var()`, `!important`, CSS-wide keywords, negative literals, `none` and `%` widths; alpha and the colour functions stay with RFC 0249. A library's tokens file is never evaluated: its form is judged from text and `tokens.ts` receives it as a module built from the literal's data. Library contrast is found by `look` keys, once per pair and scheme (k.a11y-static #11). A disabled roving `button` or `switch` writes `aria-disabled`, stays focusable and takes no click; arrows follow the computed direction. No part of the grammar ships to a client. No new code. Four more map titles were shortened to fit (RFC 0246 restores them). Its conformance-writer runs; then its builder; then the merge, with the cli suite whole.

Two things follow: the briefs say an agent waits on a file or an exit code it owns, never on `pgrep -f` of a string its own command holds, and ends every loop it starts before it reports; and tests that leave a temp folder behind are a defect to fix at the helpers (a lint or a harness check that a test's temp folders are gone at its end), added to the road to public.

- Checkpoint 207 of 2026-11-26: the last breaking review of `lane-server-up` (bad29a062): merge after fixes. Closed on re-run: every earlier finding; the server held 1,000 concurrent floating rejections with all answers 200, no thrown text logged and health at 503. Majors: (1) an error can be swallowed with check green: a handler returning `attempt(save)` or a `Promise<Result>`, an empty rejection handler, an empty `onError`, an unread `allSettled`, and E1452's own text leads an agent there; (2) `@keel/std-offline`'s `drain` observes `net` on the ctx for the page's life and `report()` reaches the platform only when a ctx has no observers, so every browser error in golden-1 and household is silent; (3) build and dev evaluation is unmarked: a top-level task ran 3 times in a node build, 5 in dev, 10 in a static build where each run rejected with a secret string, exit 0, nothing printed. Minors: `atEvaluation` misses a top-level call of a declared function, an IIFE, a constructor, a getter, a default parameter; promises put in collections, `!save()`, a project generic that drops, `el.onclick = async`, race losers are silent; a task started after an `await` in another task's work throws E1203 with no hint; work ignoring `signal` runs on after unmount; the simulated net ignores `req.signal`; stale `keel explain` texts; 70 bytes of server message in the browser's reactive entry. All of it is fixed in the lane, none to a backlog: its builder writes the spec first (one principle: a failure is read by code or reaches an owner that reports it, and no form discards one), then conformance, then the build. - Checkpoint 211 of 2026-11-26: the second breaking review of `lane-r-sessions` (bde57d0d3): do not merge. Closed on re-run: the junk flood, the garbage renewals, depth, modes, the Postgres role, E2013 at start. Blockers, reproduced: (B1) renewals add ledger entries with no holds check: 10 sources put 300 entries each against a cap of 64, used the whole renewal window so an honest renewal got 503, and fill the ledger in about 22 hours; (B2) the default `file` storage throws at 33,136 ledger entries (11 MB), far under the caps, after which every upload is a 500 with nothing logged and the deployable's other writes fail too: about 4,100 sources in an hour. Majors: the driver registry on the global object lets a module imported first take every recording, unseen by check; eviction deletes recordings and an operator's file with nothing logged; `KEEL_PROXIES` one too high lets a client choose its source, and left at 0 behind a proxy the whole site shares one quota. Minors: Postgres refusing a NUL after the use is spent, `pull` writing 0644, depth per store state, clock steps, the fingerprint cap junk can switch off, 4.4 KB of ticket code in every node server, nothing in `keel evidence`, the signer not public. Held: the mac and its constant-time comparison, origin and entry binding, `no-store` documents, 200 concurrent uploads admitting exactly 8, no SQL injection, loopback-only `http`.
- D210 (2026-11-26, to confirm or reverse): no third round of patches on RFC 0248. Twice a bound was added and the next unbounded thing was found; the cause is a per-ticket, per-source ledger kept in the app's storage. The one fable subagent redesigns ticket state at the root: stateless tickets with single use as a property of the archive write (a key derived from the ticket, an exclusive create), constant-size counters only, no renewal chain (a fresh ticket from a mint endpoint), no anonymous request ever writing the app's storage, the attacker's worst case stated as a formula; the driver named where check reads it and frozen before the server listens; eviction as the operator's policy, never a silent side effect of a write.

- Checkpoint 213 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0248 is redesigned at the root (`lane-r-sessions` ece80946c: RFC section 8, ADR-0277; k.tickets, std.sessions-ingest and std.sessions-archive rewritten and `proposed`), accepted. A ticket is `<minted>.<expiry>.<bytes>.<nonce>.<mac>` and the server keeps no record of it and writes nothing to `ctx.storage`; the kernel hands the action `at.ticket` and ingest writes `sessions/<at.ticket>.json`, every driver refusing an existing key, so a replay is 409 and single use is a property of the archive; `uses` and renewal are gone and `POST /__keel/ticket` mints statelessly; volume is bounded by a fixed table of in-memory counter pairs per entry and process (`hour.slots`; a slot is an HMAC of the source under an hourly random key), one admission step charged before decode. Invariant: state is 8 × slots + 40 bytes (4,136 for sessions); no anonymous request writes app storage; one process admits at most 2,048 recordings and 512 MiB an hour; N sources get at most 4 × min(N, 512) recordings; an honest visitor's upload is refused with chance 1 − (511/512)^N for that hour. Not held, stated: about 3,200 sources stop ingest for everyone while they send; bounds are per process; a rate limit in front is assumed for CPU. The driver is one top-level `sessionsArchive(...)` statement check reads (E2014), and a library cannot name one; Keel deletes nothing: the folder driver refuses at `maxBytes` or under `minFree` (503, W2014); a wrong `KEEL_PROXIES` costs fairness only (W1595, W1596 hourly; compose requires the variable); `pull` writes 0600; `recordings` in `pii.json`. Deleted at the build: the ticket ledger, holds, renewal, refusals, `sessions.seen`, the named-driver registry, folder eviction. The conformance-writer is next when a slot frees (ten agents run: over the staffing rule by the two integrators' tail; nothing more starts until three finish). RFC 0251's slice 2 conformance is complete (`lane-r-boundary` d8f350449: the 41 shell cases on a stub library named by `package:`; `ptyRun` proven on macOS and in a Linux container) and its builder builds slice 2 in eleven steps.

To verify at the build: that a std package can add `recordings` to `keel evidence` through a public seam; if not, the seam is designed, not worked around.

- Checkpoint 214 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0255 is written and accepted (`lane-r-fresh` fad806fea; ADR-0293; new section k.derived, 22 clauses; W1018): no reader trusts a derived file's bytes on disk. Reproduced in a built copy of `apps/household`: wrong reds (E1005 "run keel build" after renaming a page id, adding a route, changing a contract path, adding a store field, editing `topology.ts`; with `--base` the real E1563 hidden behind it; `keel test` running 0 tests when `.keel/` is read-only) and wrong greens (with no `surface.json`, a store change that breaks stored states passes `check --base`; a library's schema narrowed passes check, since `surface.json`'s `sources` lists project files only); check beside a running dev server disagrees with it. The design: each reader takes the derivation of its tree and the files become views; every run that is not frozen rewrites views whose bytes differ by atomic rename (one trailer count, `2 refreshed`; W1018 if unwritable); frozen (`--frozen`, `CI` set, `KEEL_FROZEN=1`) writes nothing and a differing view is E1005; `routes.json` and `surface.json` come from one evaluation cached under `node_modules/.cache/keel/derived/` keyed by every file loaded; `surface.json` version 3 has no `sources`; a digest inside committed files was rejected (anyone can compute it). Bound: at most 100 ms added when nothing changed, 1.5 s after an edit that affects routes. The skill lines telling an agent to build before check are deleted by the build. RFC 0247's conformance is written (`lane-r-create` c53b59bec: 67 cases failing as unbuilt; open: `createPlan`'s shapes unstated, no seam for "no network", clause 22 against k.library-pack on `keel pack --check`). RFC 0248's conformance-writer runs on the stateless design.

- D214 (2026-11-26, to confirm or reverse): E1210 is withdrawn from RFC 0245. Requiring task work that awaits to read its signal refuses correct work that awaits something taking no signal and pushes authors to a ceremonial `signal.throwIfAborted()`. What holds is the kernel's guarantee: after its owner stops, a task's results are dropped and its signal is aborted; work that should stop early reads the signal.

- Checkpoint 225 of 2026-11-26: the breaking review of `lane-r-e2e`: do not merge as is. Blocker: the inspector's server listens on every interface and its target parameter reaches arbitrary local services (`?url=http://127.0.0.1:47911/admin/delete-all%23` made a stand-in service receive that request; through the LAN address and with a foreign `Origin` too; nothing authenticates a caller; error texts reveal open ports). Minors: E1736 not firing for a page outside the library; served servers inheriting the developer's environment; the shipped loopback key trusted for every host; leftovers after SIGINT and SIGKILL; a non-Keel error printed under `fix:`; no verbs for upload, download, hover, drag, clipboard, iframe, popup or dialog and no skill line on the way through; Chromium installed in all seven shards. Held: failure output, loops five of five at load 15 to 27, two runs at once in one folder, no orphan processes, no wall-time sleeps, an app with no e2e file paying one second. The RFC's writer settles all of it in the spec first: a loopback bind for every dev-time server, a `problems()` form that reading cannot empty, a mutation recorded for each migrated assertion, a structure digest in `resume()` so the last smoke can go. Pushed: the main line with `lane-tooling-fixes` (05213d92f); CI running.

- Checkpoint 251 of 2026-11-26: RFC 0242 is redesigned on the wall test (`lane-r-defect` d948b315a; RFC decisions D21 to D40, ADR-0264; new sections k.attempt, k.defects, k.fixes, k.identity, k.shared, std.postgres-errors; codes E1457, E1458, E1260, E1596, E1764, R1017), accepted. The model: an expected failure is a `KeelError` in a `Result<T, E extends KeelError>`; anything thrown is unexpected. `attempt(fn, expect)` with `expect` required: a value is `ok`, a returned `Result` comes back flattened, a defect passes on untouched, anything else thrown goes to `expect`, which returns a `KeelError` for what it claims or `undefined` to rethrow the thrown value itself. `err` takes a `KeelError` only. `X.causedBy(cause, data)` wraps and `X.in(e)` finds a typed error on the chain, so a conflict stays retryable three frames up. `defect` takes a literal message, with data and cause as typed arguments. The five wall cases end as short code with their fix chains written into k.fixes (retry: a loop over `attempt` with an `expect` for the network error; rollback: `try/finally` with a flag, the thrown value unchanged; a throwing SDK: `attempt` with an `expect` naming the SDK's error and a closed `reason`; JSON: the schema's `parseJson` and a `switch` on the error's code; cleanup: no diagnostic), with five more chains. The autofix rule: a fix only if its output compiles in place, behaves the same and leaves nothing behind; offered for three shapes and nothing else. Shared state: a write to any node a module root owns during a request is a defect, by owner. Identity: a kernel record in the realm, made values frozen, copies refused, schemas read from the maker's record; E1762 by five proofs. Production logs never hold thrown text (a code, a site, a keyed fingerprint, the cause chain's codes; text only in dev or for at most 24 hours on an operator's opt-in). The PGlite engine reads the effective timeout by value after every statement. `Secret`'s statics are gone. Its conformance-writer runs; then a fresh builder deletes the old fixes, the symbol-key marks and the text match and migrates about 150 `attempt` calls and 40 `defect` messages.

- Checkpoint 303 of 2026-11-27: the evaluation's realm is designed (`lane-r-fresh` f0e56b51c; new section k.evaluator, ADR-0293; writing only): every build-time evaluation (`tokens.ts` and the tree) runs in one Node child per bundle, never in a command's process; the child starts with `--permission` (file read on the bundle's folder only), `--disallow-code-generation-from-strings`, `--zero-fill-buffers`, an empty working folder and an environment of `TZ`, `LANG`, `LC_*` only; its global is prepared from `HOST_VIEWS` (no proxy, no `define`); views hold none of Node's objects and ask the command over an inherited pipe, the command reading, answering and recording, so its answers are the key; the command kills the child at its result; 30 s without one or an unsettled top-level await is new code E1048, any other early exit E1049; diagnostics cross as records. This is RFC 0251's evaluator built once here (`evaluate` in `@keel/compiler`, the signature its k.machine names; its step 7 is unbuilt on `lane-r-boundary`, which later adds `prerender`, grants and the loader hook), so `lane-r-fresh` no longer waits on that lane or on RFC 0242 and 0245. F1 becomes impossible, F2's returned objects are the view's classes, F3's intrinsics die with the child, F4's timers end with it. Measured: a child start to exit 33 to 37 ms, 200 questions 4 to 6 ms; estimated warm check +35 ms. The SSR reload's "id already in use" is a separate bug (a store made at top level under a scope never disposed, re-run by `reloadDev`), builder step 9. Seventeen Conformance bullets. Open, as operator items 8 to 11 in the RFC: four meeting points with RFC 0251's text (E1050 against its E1184 and placement, whether `data` env reads are answered, glossary wording), settled when `lane-r-boundary` next merges the main line; the Node floor becomes 22.13 or later; leftover timers end silently; the 30 s deadline has no flag; Windows unverified; a command killed with SIGKILL may leave the child (the builder is to make the child exit when its pipe closes).
